† No dedicated ARM hash backend exists in this SMHasher3 tree; generic code may still use shared ARM arithmetic helpers. I retain the backend tokens verbatim. A blank means no named token; UMASH/CLHASH’s hwclmul token runs PMULL on ARM, and aHash’s portable names its shuffle while its AES rounds use ARM instructions. Hover a speed for its token and run spread.

The corrected pass retains pre-existing verification-code failures for NMHASH/NMHASHX on M2 Pro only: SMHasher3's scalar build there computes different functions (NMHASHX 0x9CF42B2D instead of 0xA8580227), so the M2 numbers for these two rows time implementations whose output is not the registered hash. The Xeon (AVX-512) builds verify (NMHASHX 0xA8580227 PASS); on x86 the upstream nmhash.h scalar path (NMH_VECTOR=0) also gives 0xA8580227. Four poly-mersenne registrations also retain their separately recorded verification-code failures; the structural Sanity checks passed. Timing alone is not a correctness certificate.

Public records index: papers, specifications, measurements, and exact certificates.

The verification package contains 29 C11 collision-reproduction packages, including MuseAir v2, Go’s map hash, Abseil Hash, Marvin32, both foldhash variants, wyhash, rapidhash v1 and XXH3-64/128. Each checks reference outputs and explicit witnesses, then samples deterministic pseudorandom seeds. The checks support implementation consistency; they do not prove equivalence on every input.

# From the page/archive parent directory:
make -C verify -j2
make -C verify -j1 check

The 27 Makefile checks reproduced their README counts on 19 September 2026. The two foldhash packages are built separately, using the commands in their READMEs; both passed their reference checks and gave zero hits in their deterministic 220-seed smoke runs (fast instructions, quality instructions). These small samples do not replace the historical large measurements or exhaustive counts. Statistical interpretations assume that the sampled streams behave like independent uniform draws; a zero count is not a hard population bound.

The a5hash supplement checks the selected 6615-byte and 17-byte witnesses. The MuseAir supplement checks the C–E witnesses, samples the BFast constructions, and counts its masked seed class exactly. Their READMEs give the commands. The per-hash packages record which selected and supporting pairs they check; a single package can cover several output variants. These checks do not re-establish the historical large counts or HighwayHash’s analytical numerator. verify.tar.gz contains the deployed source package. The interactive chart and downloadable figures are generated from the same data.json record; the static figure remains available without JavaScript.

Prior work and acknowledgements

Five entries build on earlier breaks: CityHash, FarmHash, MurmurHash3, gxhash and MuseAir. Peters’ 2024 article, the 29C3 2012 Aumasson–Bernstein–Bosslet work, and Peters’ two later-linked issue reports supply that history. MuseAir’s issue is from 2026, not the 2024 article. I use NEW when the supplied record identifies no earlier instance of that mechanism. I make no claim of exhaustive historical priority.

Changelog

Scores change as attacks and proofs improve. A lower heuristic score means a stronger example was found; a changed proven score means a bound was corrected or tightened. A hash that removes the weakness behind a demotion can move back up: each score reflects the best example known on the date below, not a permanent verdict. Each entry gives the reason and links to the reproduction. Corrections are welcome on Twitter or in the verification repository’s issue tracker.

29 September 2026

HalftimeHash24: the shipped advanced function (at most 39.4 bits) is removed from the chart and table; the row with the repaired encoder (≥ 96 bits) is now listed as HalftimeHash24.

Zen 4 tab added. The chart has a third host, an AMD EPYC 9R14 (Zen 4), with every timed row rerun through the timing reproduction (records and raw runs). ChainHash-128 cells refreshed to repository a0116ea on all hosts, and ChainHash with them, so every tab shows the same revision: Xeon 14.43 → 14.90 and M2 10.26 → 16.85 B/cycle for ChainHash-128, M2 26.26 → 30.15 for ChainHash, and shorter 1–31 B costs on Xeon. Only the speed changed: the verification values, and so the functions and their bounds, are the same (same-binary refresh).

28 September 2026

A re-analysis of every row with new search and verification programs. Every changed number below was reproduced by at least two independently written programs; the programs and logs are in records/witness-searches/reanalysis-2026-09.

Demoted (a stronger adversarial example was found):

  • t1ha2: 29.19 → 3.59 bits. A three-block top-bit trail in the 32-byte loop collides for all seeds except one interval of 2−8 of them, at any position of a long input; switching blocks gives 65,536 inputs that collide for about 94% of seeds. Now flooding-grade. NEW; reported to the maintainer by email.
  • MuseAir v2: 19.45 → 3.32 bits. An 80-byte pair on the long-input path collides for every seed by exchanging two products; hash128 is not affected. NEW, extending pair D of museair#4.
  • XXH3-64: 28.5 → 21.6 bits (a 5- vs 8-byte pair) and XXH3-128: 28.6 → 21.0 bits (a 9- vs 16-byte pair, full 128-bit), by cancelling the length term of the short paths. Both also move from pairs only to weak-key multicollision: above 240 bytes one 2−32 condition on a single secret word makes a fixed set of any size collide. NEW; the long-input family extends our earlier stripe-swap pair, and our 19 September record, which put cross-length short-path pairs near 2−64, now carries a correction.

Added rows:

  • XXH64 (≤ 1 bit; key-free 16-byte pair, flooding-grade) and XXH32 (0.00 bits; an 8-byte pair for all but 215 of 232 seeds, flooding-grade). NEW.
  • MurmurHash64A, used by libstdc++ for std::hash<std::string> (0 bits; a key-free 8- vs 7-byte pair, EXTENSION), and MurmurHash2 and MurmurHash2A (0 bits). Their key-free flooding sets reproduce Aumasson, Bernstein and Boßlet (29C3, 2012).

Multicollision tiers (note):

  • nmhash32: weak-key → flooding-grade. A fixed set of 65,536 inputs keeps one eighth of itself in one class for every seed. NEW.
  • aHash: pairs only → few-way (4 inputs for about 2−12.2 of keys, 16 for 2−24.3). NEW.
  • Shorter every-seed flooding sets: CityHash64 and FarmHash64 32 → 16 bytes; mx3, MUM, mir and Abseil 16 → 10 bytes; MuseAir v0.3 32 → 18 bytes; a5hash-128 24 → 18 bytes; MurmurHash3 256 → 144 bytes; gxhash 18 bytes, the shortest for its construction. mx3, MUM and mir have key-free pairs at every length from 9 bytes. Mostly EXTENSIONs of earlier constructions.
  • Long inputs: CityHash64 and FarmHash64 have a key-free rotation differential in any loop block (NEW); Abseil has every-seed families in every build: CRC32C subspaces at 9–32 bytes in the opt-in hardware build (reported in abseil-cpp#2171), a 64-byte reset block in the scalar build and piece-start cancellations in the Apple-silicon AES build (NEW). nmhash32, .NET Marvin and t1ha2 differentials work at any position up to 1 MB; SpookyHash’s works after any prefix but only in the last block; HalftimeHash24’s key condition frees every leaf. Negative results: the bulk loops of MuseAir v0.3 and v2 and of komihash past one block hold against these methods.
  • komihash: a 73-byte pair that collides for every seed (3.32 bits, so the 3.14-bit score is unchanged).

Corrections (a bound or a label was wrong or understated):

  • HalftimeHash24, repaired encoder: 83.27 → ≥ 96 bits. The certified normalized score in our own theorem was already 96, attained by 00/01; the plotted value was the conservative envelope label.
  • HalftimeHash24, shipped: 36.39 → 39.39 bits. The 168-byte pair belongs to advanced::V1<3>; the row names and times V4<3>, whose witness is 1344 bytes long. A scope correction, not a stronger hash.
  • UMASH-128: the plotted 83.99 stands. Only the published headline, 83.0 bits, is machine-checked in Lean; the extra bit is the paper’s exact-arithmetic envelope. umash_params_prepare samples exactly the theorems’ key distribution, so our “nonuniform sampler” caveat is withdrawn.
  • PolymurHash: the plotted ≥ 54.23 is the ideal-key theorem. Under the shipped seeding a fixed pair collides with probability 2−53.90 exactly (NEW).
  • Poly1305: the true score lies in [103.78, 104] (L = 1 is exactly 104); the plotted, Lean-checked 103 stands. ChainHash and ChainHash-128: the 63- and 127-bit bounds are attained at L = 1. HighwayHash: the 59.81-bit cap stands, with a note on why the known attacks stop near 2−56.
  • foldhash: the cross-type collisions are labelled out of scope by design, following the maintainer’s reply on foldhash#50.

28 September 2026, earlier

Added PolyXOR128, Orson Peters’s new 128-bit hash, as a proven row at ≥ 126.41 bits. Its Lean proof was rebuilt, the crate was checked against the Lean definitions, and it was timed in SMHasher3 on both hosts.

23 September 2026

The eight-lane recurrence row: 61 → ≥ 64 bits. Lanes that are empty at the common length cancel, so the combining polynomial has lower degree than the earlier bound assumed. The textbook Lean proofs were published; see the machine-checked results.
